## EAOSE-C001 — Affected-stakeholder validation

### Current scientific adjudication

**Name.** Affected-stakeholder validation

**Original disposition.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Native examination status.** EXAMINED_WITH_EVIDENCE_LIMIT

**New author reasoning.** The affected population can include a non-user who never appears in an operator interview. Retain fallible discovery and challenge, including qualified representation; neither an exhaustive-looking diagram nor agreement among consulted actors proves completeness. A042 contributes duplicate history, not a second validation mechanism.

**Mechanism.** Elicit and challenge the set of affected actors, including people who do not operate or purchase the system; validate goals with those actors or explicitly qualified representatives.

**Trigger or context.** Several parties can be helped, harmed or made dependent by the system.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** For a genuinely single-party local task, a short agreed requirement and counterexample review can replace an actor diagram.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Access to affected parties or a disclosed representation limit; a boundary that can be revised.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Requirements engineer and affected participants; the sponsor cannot silently speak for all parties.

**Observable postcondition.** A challenged actor inventory and acceptance criteria expose omissions and unresolved disagreement.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C01

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS044; EAOSE-CS056

**Researcher objection.** A complete diagram can omit an affected non-user; consultation can be tokenistic.

**Known failure modes.** A complete diagram can omit an affected non-user; consultation can be tokenistic.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C01

**Detail.** A complete diagram can omit an affected non-user; consultation can be tokenistic.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Reduces unjustified requirements closure; magnitude of benefit is not established generally.

**Disposition rationale.** A strongly retains the distinction that a goal model needs affected-actor validation; B retains the fallible discovery method. Canonical strong retention concerns the distinction, not proof that the procedure finds every actor. A-042 adds no active mechanism.

**Current status.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Canonical disposition.** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Mechanism.** Translate selected stakeholder objectives into observable acceptance and prohibited-outcome conditions; challenge AND/OR refinements as hypotheses about sufficiency.

**Trigger or context.** A system is to be accepted as satisfying a stakeholder goal.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A small executable example plus a human acceptance decision may suffice; not every concern needs a formal goal tree.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.

**Property dependencies.** EAOSE-C001

**Specific preconditions.** A declared observer, relevant environment and criterion; stakeholder authority for trade-offs.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Stakeholders author/approve the acceptance meaning; testers produce evidence; implementers cannot redefine success after a failure.

**Observable postcondition.** Accepted evidence distinguishes a completed local action from achievement of the intended goal.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C01; C12

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS093; EAOSE-CS047

**Researcher objection.** Good measurable proxies may omit consent, harm or long-term effects; goal satisfaction can be gamed.

**Known failure modes.** Good measurable proxies may omit consent, harm or long-term effects; goal satisfaction can be gamed.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C01; C12

**Detail.** Good measurable proxies may omit consent, harm or long-term effects; goal satisfaction can be gamed.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevents model or action completion from being accepted as outcome fulfilment without a bridge.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Both distinguish stakeholder ends, implemented goal states and observable acceptance. A adds prohibited outcomes and AND/OR sufficiency challenges; B makes the three-way semantic separation explicit. These are refinements of the same translation criterion, not three competing definitions of success.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Asset owner sets protection duties; engineers implement and test them; attacker input has no authority.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A isolates security constraints and threat-to-test traceability. B-005 combines this with delegation/capability. Preserve the security path from stakeholder concern to role/plan/interaction and prohibited-action tests without treating threat modelling as authenticated authority.

**Mechanism.** Treat latency, resource use, safety, privacy, security and adverse stakeholder consequences as requirements with explicit acceptance and conflict rules, not as optional additions to task success.

**Trigger or context.** Task achievement can cause material cost or harm, or service qualities determine acceptability.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A narrow low-risk computation can use a small explicit quality budget rather than a full assurance programme.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** Actual affected actors and their accounts; proposed goals/dependencies, constraints, legitimacy and observable acceptance information.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Stakeholder participation, measurable or evaluable criteria, hard-versus-negotiable distinction and evidence suited to each constraint.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Legitimate stakeholders and responsible authorities set constraints; agents cannot trade away non-delegable limits merely because a utility score improves.

**Observable postcondition.** A successful task is accepted only under the applicable consequence and quality constraints, with unresolved trade-offs exposed.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C12; C20; C23

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS093; EAOSE-CS087; EAOSE-CS111

**Researcher objection.** Proxy metrics omit affected parties; averaging can hide catastrophic failures. A norm-aware planner's willingness to violate a soft norm does not authorise violating a hard access boundary.

**Known failure modes.** Proxy metrics omit affected parties; averaging can hide catastrophic failures. A norm-aware planner's willingness to violate a soft norm does not authorise violating a hard access boundary.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C12; C20; C23

**Detail.** Proxy metrics omit affected parties; averaging can hide catastrophic failures. A norm-aware planner's willingness to violate a soft norm does not authorise violating a hard access boundary.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevent local task performance from concealing unacceptable consequences.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A separately extracts quality/resource/adverse-consequence constraints. B goal alternatives and security cover selected instances, not this full residual engineering obligation; those related records are not falsely equated.

**Mechanism.** Partition large requirements models into views with explicit interfaces and cross-view consistency responsibilities.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Keep one small model when splitting would only add navigation and synchronisation costs.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Requirements team partitions; affected participants can still see relevant cross-boundary effects.

**Observable postcondition.** Users can reason locally without losing consequential inter-view dependencies.

**Known failure modes.** Modularity can hide omitted stakeholders or inconsistent goal refinements.

**Detail.** Modularity can hide omitted stakeholders or inconsistent goal refinements.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Preserves usability of requirements modelling without claiming smaller is always better.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Modular requirements views with declared interfaces address representation size and cross-view understanding. B has iStar and learnability evidence, but no independent row for this modularisation mechanism. This is an explicit property-population difference, not proof B ignored the topic.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Ask an omitted-actor or false-success question before adding any diagram.

**Interpretation.** Guarded informational/semantic dependencies, not a mandatory serial pipeline; equivalent adequate inputs may substitute.

**Other preconditions.** Participants supply needs, exclusions and priorities; software does not acquire permission merely by representing them. Models can be internally complete over an incorrectly chosen boundary.

**Actor and authority boundary.** **Actors.** Requirements analyst with affected actors and the legitimate requirements decision maker

**Boundary.** Participants supply needs, exclusions and priorities; software does not acquire permission merely by representing them.

**Observable postcondition.** **Criterion.** Diagram completeness is never reported as demonstrated completeness of real needs.

**How to examine.** Inspect the actual decision/behaviour/evidence at the trigger; challenge it with the candidate criticism and relevant analytical cases. An artefact’s presence alone is not fulfilment.

**Important criticisms.** **Ledger ids.** CR-01

**Local analytical objection.** Models can be internally complete over an incorrectly chosen boundary.

**Attribution.** Ledger entries distinguish sourced objections from the researcher’s adversarial strengthening.

**Known failure modes.** Models can be internally complete over an incorrectly chosen boundary.; The mechanism is applied outside its trigger or its output has no actual decision consumer.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** CR-01

**Evidence unit ids.** EV-07

**Limit or counterexample.** Models can be internally complete over an incorrectly chosen boundary.

**No negative effect size inferred.** True

**Open questions.** **Research or transfer limit.** Models can be internally complete over an incorrectly chosen boundary.

**Whole method benefit.** No retention disposition resolves EAOSE-C120.

**Next discriminating evidence.** A faithful comparison or counterexample under the declared trigger, with the same relevant requirements and measured burden, can alter this local judgement.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. The rejected premise concerns completeness of stakeholder requirements, not correct implementation of a completed model (A-108). Preserve the separate false inference and its missing-actor witness.

**Observable postcondition.** Stakeholder concepts remain traceable in a conventional implementation without invented agent processes.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** None recorded.

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS015

**Researcher objection.** Agent terminology can obscure an ordinary service design; dropping the runtime can also lose genuinely needed autonomy.

**Known failure modes.** Agent terminology can obscure an ordinary service design; dropping the runtime can also lose genuinely needed autonomy.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** None recorded.

**Detail.** Agent terminology can obscure an ordinary service design; dropping the runtime can also lose genuinely needed autonomy.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Preserves useful analysis while avoiding unnecessary platform costs.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Both permit agent-oriented requirements/design concepts without commitment to an agent runtime. This is a configuration boundary, not rejection of agent-oriented analysis.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. **Actor and authority boundary.** Authorised organisational controller or participating principals approve changes; not every agent has reorganisation rights.

**Observable postcondition.** A reconfiguration leaves each live obligation fulfilled, transferred or explicitly unresolved.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C03

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS022; EAOSE-CS055

**Researcher objection.** Thrashing, temporary coverage gaps and inconsistent memberships may defeat the intended adaptation.

**Known failure modes.** Thrashing, temporary coverage gaps and inconsistent memberships may defeat the intended adaptation.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C03

**Detail.** Thrashing, temporary coverage gaps and inconsistent memberships may defeat the intended adaptation.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Enables adaptation without silently importing dynamic guarantees into static methods.

**Disposition rationale.** Use assumption-sensitive: valid allocation depends on capabilities, organisational constraints and sufficiently known state. Mere context relevance is weaker than discharging those premises.

Norm-aware BDI planning can consider conformity and violation consequences, but must not rewrite an externally imposed permission into a preference.

**Trigger or context.** Open organisations or applications with consequential social obligations, sanctions or rules whose violation must remain visible.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A hard access rule or a simple externally enforced contract may need no autonomous normative reasoning. A conventional policy table can suffice.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Norm issuers and affected principals establish obligations; agents decide only within delegated discretion; monitors and responsible parties consume violation evidence.

**Observable postcondition.** The applicable norm, responsible subject and fulfilment or violation outcome can be established for a concrete interaction, including unresolved evidence.

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS037; EAOSE-CS099

**Researcher objection.** Conflicting norms, unverifiable fulfilment, dynamic norm changes and incentives can defeat local conformity. The 2026 Jason example demonstrates a mechanism, not scalable or globally compliant deployment.

**Known failure modes.** Conflicting norms, unverifiable fulfilment, dynamic norm changes and incentives can defeat local conformity. The 2026 Jason example demonstrates a mechanism, not scalable or globally compliant deployment.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C18; C23

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Make obligations and permissible responses inspectable without mistaking cooperation for universal compliance.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Norm activation, violation and recovery are not identical to hard permission enforcement or to a binary safety invariant. B organisational/commitment records contain neighbours but no equivalent full norm-lifecycle candidate.

**Mechanism.** Separate agent-internal behaviour, organisational constraints and environment-resource behaviour where they have different change and authority boundaries; maintain explicit interfaces between these dimensions.

**Trigger or context.** Systems where roles, norms, resource operations and individual plans evolve independently.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A small cohesive programme may keep these concerns together when the distinctions remain clear and modular separation adds no value.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** Role/organisation rules, principals/capabilities, runtime bindings, goals, live obligations and the current reconfiguration context.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Agent, organisation and resource designers may be the same person, but their authority and change obligations are distinguished; operators consume the composed behaviour.

**Observable postcondition.** An organisational or resource change has identifiable effects on agent behaviour rather than silently changing several meanings at once.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** None recorded.

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS078; EAOSE-CS112

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** None recorded.

**Detail.** Separate modules can still share hidden state; administrative APIs may bypass organisational constraints. Conceptual dimensions are not automatically separate security domains.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Support selective evolution and diagnosis without imposing one process or owner for each conceptual dimension.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A separately preserves agent, organisation and environment dimensions and their interfaces. B environment and role properties cover two concerns without an equivalent separation-of-dimensions criterion.

**Researcher objection.** FIPA message structure alone does not authenticate identity. Shared credentials, replay and ambiguous principal mapping undermine accountability.

**Known failure modes.** FIPA message structure alone does not authenticate identity. Shared credentials, replay and ambiguous principal mapping undermine accountability.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C04; C20

**Detail.** FIPA message structure alone does not authenticate identity. Shared credentials, replay and ambiguous principal mapping undermine accountability.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevent mistaken delegation or action on impersonated requests.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Authentication binds messages to principals and is not supplied by an ontology, sender field, protocol binding or action permission. B criticises some confusions but lacks a separate positive authentication candidate.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevent accidental protocol progress from unreceived or miscorrelated information.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A separately treats correlation, delayed/lost/duplicate communication and uncertainty of delivery. B-021 and B-025 overlap in meaning and recovery, but do not eliminate this transport/delivery residual.

**Local analytical objection.** A method’s support for BDI does not make BDI obligatory or sufficient for every autonomous component.

**Attribution.** Ledger entries distinguish sourced objections from the researcher’s adversarial strengthening.

**Known failure modes.** A method’s support for BDI does not make BDI obligatory or sufficient for every autonomous component.; The mechanism is applied outside its trigger or its output has no actual decision consumer.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** CR-06

**Evidence unit ids.** None recorded.

**Limit or counterexample.** A method’s support for BDI does not make BDI obligatory or sufficient for every autonomous component.

**No negative effect size inferred.** True

**Open questions.** **Research or transfer limit.** A method’s support for BDI does not make BDI obligatory or sufficient for every autonomous component.

**Whole method benefit.** No retention disposition resolves EAOSE-C120.

**Next discriminating evidence.** A faithful comparison or counterexample under the declared trigger, with the same relevant requirements and measured burden, can alter this local judgement.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. B explicitly selects among deliberative, reactive and conventional internal mechanisms; A retains BDI where deliberation warrants it. Canonicalise architecture choice with BDI as the supported guarded variant, not an obligation for every agent. The broader option set comes from B, not invented extension of A.

**Current status.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Canonical disposition.** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Mechanism.** Associate decision-relevant beliefs with observations or derivations, update and defeat conditions, and unresolved uncertainty. Do not promote an agent's stored belief into an external fact simply because it has a symbolic label or a fluent explanation.

**Trigger or context.** Agents whose observations become stale, conflict or are derived from fallible models.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A deterministic local computation over immutable, validated input may require only ordinary input checks rather than a belief-provenance subsystem.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Observable input boundaries, update policy, relevant freshness criteria and a way to distinguish absence from falsity.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Agent designers define belief-update semantics; operators or dependent decision-makers consume the stated evidence and uncertainty; external facts are not determined by the agent.

**Observable postcondition.** A consequential belief can be traced to an inspected observation or derivation and revised or withheld when its support fails.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C21

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS085; EAOSE-CS113

**Researcher objection.** Unlogged derivations, ambiguous sensors and learned summaries break reconstruction; provenance does not prove truth. Full provenance can be expensive and privacy-sensitive.

**Known failure modes.** Unlogged derivations, ambiguous sensors and learned summaries break reconstruction; provenance does not prove truth. Full provenance can be expensive and privacy-sensitive.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C21

**Detail.** Unlogged derivations, ambiguous sensors and learned summaries break reconstruction; provenance does not prove truth. Full provenance can be expensive and privacy-sensitive.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Reduce action based on stale or invented premises and make assumption failures diagnosable.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A separately treats belief source, revision and observational uncertainty. This is neither a guarantee that memory is true nor identical to validating a sensor-to-belief mapping; evidence of particular beliefs can fail even with an otherwise sound interface.

**Canonical disposition.** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Mechanism.** Retain an adopted intention long enough to coordinate useful action, with explicit reconsideration when achievement, impossibility, irrelevance or competing obligations warrant it; account for the cost of reconsideration itself.

**Trigger or context.** Long-lived, interruptible or resource-bounded goal pursuit where continual replanning or blind persistence causes harm.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A one-shot deterministic operation may need no persistent intention state; a standard job lifecycle can implement the necessary persistence.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Defined achievement and abandonment conditions, observation of relevant changes, resource bounds and authority to change commitments.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Agent designers choose reconsideration policy within stakeholder priorities; the acting agent cannot silently abandon public commitments merely by dropping an internal intention.

**Observable postcondition.** The system can explain why pursuit continued, was suspended or was abandoned, and what happened to dependent obligations.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C22

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS122; EAOSE-CS050

**Researcher objection.** Overcommitment wastes resources; excessive reconsideration thrashes. Philosophical and formal motivation does not establish an optimal policy in every environment.

**Known failure modes.** Overcommitment wastes resources; excessive reconsideration thrashes. Philosophical and formal motivation does not establish an optimal policy in every environment.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C22

**Detail.** Overcommitment wastes resources; excessive reconsideration thrashes. Philosophical and formal motivation does not establish an optimal policy in every environment.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Preserve useful continuity without making persistence unconditional.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A extracts bounded reconsideration and useful intention persistence. B concurrency/interference is a different scheduling problem; a stable intention policy can still interfere with another intention.

**Observable postcondition.** Each active goal has a legitimate origin and explicit lifecycle state, with satisfaction or abandonment justified by the appropriate evidence.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. **Mechanism.** Specify priority, interruption, resumption and arbitration between reactive handlers and deliberative plans rather than assuming their coexistence yields a coherent controller.

**Trigger or context.** Urgent events can arrive during planning or multiple behaviours compete for shared resources.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A purely reactive controller or a sequential job processor may dominate where goals do not require deliberative persistence.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Event semantics, shared-resource policy, interruption points, maximum tolerable delay and recovery from partially executed plans.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Designers and relevant safety or operations authorities establish precedence; the runtime executes the specified arbitration rather than inventing authority.

**Observable postcondition.** A triggered urgent event is handled within the declared scope, with the interrupted task and effects accounted for.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C22

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS122; EAOSE-CS017

**Researcher objection.** Reactive starvation, deliberative blocking and omitted reasoning overhead defeat informal timeliness claims; not every BDI implementation is pre-emptive.

**Known failure modes.** Reactive starvation, deliberative blocking and omitted reasoning overhead defeat informal timeliness claims; not every BDI implementation is pre-emptive.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C22

**Detail.** Reactive starvation, deliberative blocking and omitted reasoning overhead defeat informal timeliness claims; not every BDI implementation is pre-emptive.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Avoid inconsistent responses and uncontrolled interference between useful forms of behaviour.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. The bearer is arbitration between reactive and deliberative behaviour, not a waiting plan-generation operation. Preserve separately from B-083's specific non-blocking mechanism and from hard temporal feasibility.

**Mechanism.** Represent decision-relevant uncertainty and define when to gather evidence, choose a robust action, defer or abstain; do not equate a plausible plan or high model score with sufficient evidence for a consequential act.

**Trigger or context.** Partial observations, uncertain consequences, stochastic components or material model/environment mismatch.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Deterministic validated inputs and reversible low-consequence actions may justify a simpler direct policy.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Specific preconditions.** A declared consequence model, risk or acceptability threshold, feasible alternatives and a legitimate consumer of abstention or escalation.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Stakeholders establish tolerable consequences and delegated discretion; agents choose only within that boundary; humans are not assumed to be infallible or always available.

**Observable postcondition.** The decision records what uncertainty mattered and why action or abstention met the applicable criterion.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C21; C22

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS093; EAOSE-CS107

**Researcher objection.** Uncalibrated scores, unavailable humans, distribution shift and badly specified thresholds can defeat the policy. This is an analytical integration of assurance boundaries, not a uniquely AOSE theorem.

**Known failure modes.** Uncalibrated scores, unavailable humans, distribution shift and badly specified thresholds can defeat the policy. This is an analytical integration of assurance boundaries, not a uniquely AOSE theorem.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C06; C21; C22

**Detail.** Uncalibrated scores, unavailable humans, distribution shift and badly specified thresholds can defeat the policy. This is an analytical integration of assurance boundaries, not a uniquely AOSE theorem.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Reduce unjustified action while avoiding indiscriminate escalation and delay.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A identifies an uncertainty-aware decision/abstention policy rather than merely recording uncertain observations. No B row has the same decision threshold and non-action alternative.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevents timeless logical correctness from being mistaken for timely operation.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A asks whether goals/plans/actions meet a temporal envelope including reasoning cost. B-083 avoids blocking unrelated duties but does not establish deadlines. These bearers and guarantees differ.

**Mechanism.** Bound computation, messages, tokens, retries, elapsed time or other relevant resources and define what termination means for goals, obligations and already-issued effects.

**Trigger or context.** Autonomous or conversational loops can continue indefinitely or consume unacceptable resources.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A statically bounded operation may need only ordinary timeout and cost limits.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The chosen executable semantics, actual internal/environment state, candidate actions/plans, implementation artefacts and relevant resource/timing conditions.

**Actor and authority boundary.** The responsible principal sets budgets; the runtime enforces the supported limits; an agent cannot declare success simply because a budget ended.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C22

**Detail.** Token limits may depend on reported usage; checks after a response cannot prevent all overshoot. Stopping a conversation does not cancel remote effects.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Control runaway cost and make incomplete outcomes intelligible.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Runtime budgets, bounded recursion and termination/recovery conditions differ from measuring cost in an experiment. B cost-aware evaluation and slow-generation response are related but not equivalent mechanisms.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. B design-to-protocol consistency is a typed subset of A's cross-view/metamodel consistency. The canonical mechanism is parameterised by exact views and mapping; it does not transfer a result between arbitrary metamodels.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A one-off simple solution may cost less than discovering, adapting and governing a reusable asset.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The actual engineering problem, selected fragments and their inputs/outputs, usable prescriptions, team capabilities, costs and legitimate method-change rules.

**Property dependencies.** EAOSE-C034; EAOSE-C059; EAOSE-C064

**Specific preconditions.** Asset identity and version, semantic contract, known limitations and responsibility for adaptation.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Method engineers or developers select and adapt assets; prior authorship does not authorise reuse outside the evidence boundary.

**Observable postcondition.** The reused asset's assumptions and adaptations are documented to the degree required by its actual consumers.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C08

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS061; EAOSE-CS050

**Researcher objection.** Superficial similarity, stale platform mappings and omitted tacit expertise can reverse expected savings.

**Known failure modes.** Superficial similarity, stale platform mappings and omitted tacit expertise can reverse expected savings.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C08

**Detail.** Superficial similarity, stale platform mappings and omitted tacit expertise can reverse expected savings.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Capture legitimate reuse while avoiding false equivalence between branded fragments.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Same context-qualified reuse mechanism on patterns/assets and their assumptions. Reuse does not establish applicability merely because an artefact once worked.

**Mechanism.** State the assumptions each component or proof needs from other agents, protocols and the environment, and show that the composed configuration supplies compatible support before transferring a guarantee.

**Trigger or context.** Local analyses or independent components are combined into a consequential system.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Direct system-level tests and a small architecture may be cheaper than a formal assume–guarantee calculus, while still requiring explicit critical assumptions.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The exact claim, model/implementation/environment mapping, oracle, guarantee assumptions and relevant tests/observations/counterexamples.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Designers and assurance decision-makers discharge composition obligations; one component cannot certify an unexamined peer or environment.

**Observable postcondition.** Each claimed system guarantee identifies its local warrants and environmental assumptions, including any obligation not established.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C10; C25

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS122; EAOSE-CS017

**Researcher objection.** Mutually circular assumptions, hidden shared resources and unmodelled timing invalidate naïve composition. This row does not claim a universal automatic proof rule.

**Known failure modes.** Mutually circular assumptions, hidden shared resources and unmodelled timing invalidate naïve composition. This row does not claim a universal automatic proof rule.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C10; C25

**Detail.** Mutually circular assumptions, hidden shared resources and unmodelled timing invalidate naïve composition. This row does not claim a universal automatic proof rule.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevent local correctness from being mistaken for collective adequacy.

**Disposition rationale.** A-079 and B-073 instantiate important operational joins, but no residual failure beyond the inherited global compatible-warrant criterion is established. Keep their joins as EAOSE-RB01, preserve losing retained-induced judgements, and use A-100's assumption-sensitive status rather than treating local evidence as an unconditional result.

**Mechanism.** Define oracles for goal, action, protocol and prohibited-outcome behaviour; use independent observations and qualified human judgement where automation is insufficient.

**Trigger or context.** Agent output or behaviour needs a pass/fail or acceptance decision.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Simple deterministic assertions are sufficient when they capture the actual success and harm conditions.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The exact claim, model/implementation/environment mapping, oracle, guarantee assumptions and relevant tests/observations/counterexamples.

**Property dependencies.** EAOSE-C002; EAOSE-C003; EAOSE-C084

**Specific preconditions.** Requirement semantics, observable outputs/effects and a disclosed uncertainty policy.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Test designer sets oracle before judging results; system under test cannot be sole unquestioned judge.

**Observable postcondition.** Tests discriminate relevant failures including apparently successful but prohibited behaviour.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C12

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS093; EAOSE-CS047

**Researcher objection.** Final-state metrics can reward an unauthorised path; LLM judges can share biases with the system.

**Known failure modes.** Final-state metrics can reward an unauthorised path; LLM judges can share biases with the system.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C12

**Detail.** Final-state metrics can reward an unauthorised path; LLM judges can share biases with the system.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Makes testing consequential rather than demonstration-shaped.

**Disposition rationale.** B's gameable-oracle warning directly changes use: a measurable proxy can reward omitted harm or correlated evidence. Retain the oracle mechanism with this primary caution rather than average a favourable and adverse label.

**Mechanism.** Monitor selected observable runtime obligations and route violations/uncertainty to a permitted response.

**Trigger or context.** Long-lived, adaptive or open operation may depart from design assumptions.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C14

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS113; EAOSE-CS078

**Researcher objection.** Incomplete logs, delayed detection and alert fatigue can defeat monitoring; observation alone is not control.

**Known failure modes.** Incomplete logs, delayed detection and alert fatigue can defeat monitoring; observation alone is not control.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C14

**Detail.** Incomplete logs, delayed detection and alert fatigue can defeat monitoring; observation alone is not control.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Adds operational feedback to static assurance without claiming omniscience.

**Disposition rationale.** Monitoring must be proportionate to changing or uncertain operational behaviour. Retention in B does not override the non-trigger for a small directly observable stable system.

**Mechanism.** Correlate requirement-relevant decisions, messages, attempts and effects using identifiers adequate to reconstruct an interaction without conflating concurrent instances.

**Trigger or context.** Failures, assurance or accountability require tracing distributed execution.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A single ordered trace is enough for one process; do not build distributed tracing without need.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The deployed configuration, material changes, observed effects, retained evidence, live obligations, available consumers and authorised operational decisions.

**Property dependencies.** EAOSE-C034; EAOSE-C099; EAOSE-C102; EAOSE-C124

**Specific preconditions.** Trustworthy event meaning, correlation scope, clock/order assumptions and data minimisation.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Components emit evidence; operators/testers use it; logs do not authorise the actions they describe.

**Observable postcondition.** An observed effect can be related to its request/attempt and relevant configuration or labelled uncorrelated.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C14

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS101; EAOSE-CS113

**Researcher objection.** Missing events, spoofed provenance and partial clocks can create false causal narratives.

**Known failure modes.** Missing events, spoofed provenance and partial clocks can create false causal narratives.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C14

**Detail.** Missing events, spoofed provenance and partial clocks can create false causal narratives.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Supports bounded diagnosis and conformance rather than unlimited observability.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. Same cross-agent/configuration/time correlation mechanism. Correlation supplies evidence for diagnosis but does not itself prove a cause; the separate localisation property keeps that ceiling visible.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Supports targeted repairs rather than framework-wide unsupported blame.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A separately infers/classifies a failure using traces. B-071 supplies correlated traces; inference quality, alternative explanations and localisation scope are the distinct residual.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** No revalidation is needed for a proved irrelevant change; a targeted regression may suffice.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The deployed configuration, material changes, observed effects, retained evidence, live obligations, available consumers and authorised operational decisions.

**Property dependencies.** EAOSE-C002; EAOSE-C004; EAOSE-C067; EAOSE-C080; EAOSE-C082; EAOSE-C102; EAOSE-C104; EAOSE-C124

**Specific preconditions.** Dependency information and the distinction between invalidated support and falsified conclusion.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Authorised change owner and assurance consumer decide scope; successor implementation does not warrant itself.

**Observable postcondition.** Affected claims are re-supported, narrowed, suspended or withdrawn; independent warrants are not erased.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C15

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS050; EAOSE-CS122

**Researcher objection.** Blind full re-audits waste effort; incomplete dependency models miss invalidated evidence.

**Known failure modes.** Blind full re-audits waste effort; incomplete dependency models miss invalidated evidence.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C15

**Detail.** Blind full re-audits waste effort; incomplete dependency models miss invalidated evidence.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Maintains assurance continuity without treating old results as timeless certificates.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A-053 and B-057 are the same selective support-revalidation mechanism. B-006 specialises the changed premise to goals; B-070 to models/prompts/remote services. A-080 and B-074 are explicit duplicates. Changed assumptions can withdraw applicability/support without logically falsifying the conclusion; unaffected warrants remain usable.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A direct conversation plus maintained configuration may be adequate in a small team.

**Disposition rationale.** A-056 already contains a legitimate usable consumer; A-081 sharpens reachability/capacity but adds no distinct failure-prevention criterion. Retain the evolved handoff with its consequential-evidence trigger.

B explicitly assembles operational ownership, resource access and legacy-service integration obligations beyond runnable code. A manual-obligation inventory, configuration and topology are neighbouring mechanisms, not a substitute for tested integration and an operating owner.

**Mechanism.** Choose deployment boundaries and communication mechanisms according to locality, trust, failure isolation, resource constraints and organisational control, rather than mapping each conceptual role to a separate process.

**Trigger or context.** Distribution changes what can fail, who controls state or which guarantees interactions can rely on.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A single process or conventional service can be the correct deployment for several conceptual roles.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The deployed configuration, material changes, observed effects, retained evidence, live obligations, available consumers and authorised operational decisions.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Architects and infrastructure owners establish deployable boundaries; principals retain the responsibilities that process layout does not erase.

**Observable postcondition.** Each consequential interaction has an actual path and trust/failure boundary consistent with its specified semantics.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C05; C24

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS036; EAOSE-CS078

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A isolates placement/topology and trust-boundary choices. B-053 integration is broader operational coverage but does not replace this specific deployment-design mechanism.

**Eaose-s056.** **Locator.** Layered validation; section 5 May 1999 flight events and recovery decisions

**Inspected scope.** SELECTED_FULL_TEXT

**Eaose-s027.** **Locator.** Information protocols, dynamic role binding, enactment and evaluation

**Inspected scope.** SELECTED_FULL_TEXT

**Claim boundary.** Protocol-level causality and binding do not grant authority, authenticate principals or prove business success.

**Current status.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Canonical disposition.** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Mechanism.** On retries, resumed executions or uncertain responses, distinguish not-issued, issued, acknowledged and externally completed effects; use idempotent operations, deduplication, status reconciliation or compensation according to the external contract.

**Trigger or context.** Tool calls or distributed actions can outlive a timeout, interruption or process restart.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** A pure computation or an atomic in-process operation with no surviving external effect may need no extra deduplication mechanism.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The deployed configuration, material changes, observed effects, retained evidence, live obligations, available consumers and authorised operational decisions.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Effect identity, external status evidence, retry contract, appropriate storage and authority for compensation.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Implementers and external resource owners establish effect semantics; operators resolve ambiguous outcomes within their authority.

**Observable postcondition.** A retry cannot silently produce an unrecognised duplicate consequence; uncertain outcomes remain explicit until reconciled.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C15; C25

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS117; EAOSE-CS093

**Researcher objection.** A checkpointer is not an exactly-once guarantee; code before an interrupt may replay. Compensation may be impossible or harmful after irreversible effects.

**Known failure modes.** A checkpointer is not an exactly-once guarantee; code before an interrupt may replay. Compensation may be impossible or harmful after irreversible effects.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C15; C25

**Detail.** A checkpointer is not an exactly-once guarantee; code before an interrupt may replay. Compensation may be impossible or harmful after irreversible effects.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Prevent false completion and repeated side effects across otherwise valid control transitions.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A identifies crash/restart/uncertain-effect reconciliation. B exceptional interaction recovery is broader and does not establish idempotent effects or exactly-once semantics. Preserve this source-grounded contemporary operational residual.

**Mechanism.** Provide meaningful participation and intervention to affected or responsible humans, including what they observe, decide and can actually stop or change; separate consultation, approval and cancellation of ongoing effects.

**Non trigger or cheap path.** Low-risk, fully specified reversible operations may not need human approval at every step; prior policy can be sufficient.

**Dependencies or preconditions.** **Data.** The deployed configuration, material changes, observed effects, retained evidence, live obligations, available consumers and authorised operational decisions.

**Property dependencies.** None recorded.

**Specific preconditions.** Identifiable decision rights, understandable evidence, workable response times and tested intervention semantics.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Affected participants validate relevant needs; authorised operators approve or interrupt within their rights; the agent cannot manufacture approval from conversational agreement.

**Observable postcondition.** A human decision reaches the execution boundary and its effect or inability to halt an action is observable.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C16

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS093; EAOSE-CS117

**Detail.** Approval fatigue, unavailable operators, restarted nodes and already-issued external actions can make nominal oversight ineffective.

**Open questions.** Under what actual conditions does this mechanism improve its proposed payoff enough to justify its cost? Preserve legitimate participation and usable control rather than ceremonial human presence.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A tests whether an authorised human intervention actually interrupts or constrains consequential effects, including already-issued work. B authority/revision rules do not by themselves supply effective interruption.

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS078; EAOSE-CS017

**Researcher objection.** Self-improvement rhetoric can hide policy bypass, regression and loss of traceability.

**Known failure modes.** Self-improvement rhetoric can hide policy bypass, regression and loss of traceability.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C16

**Detail.** Self-improvement rhetoric can hide policy bypass, regression and loss of traceability.

**Open questions.** The universal or sufficient-condition claim is not warranted; its rejection does not reject all bounded uses of the underlying mechanism.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A rejects unmonitored self-modification as authorised evolution. B-082 rejects observation/self-revision as guaranteed improvement. Legitimate permission and beneficial outcome are distinct conclusions, so the negative candidates cannot merge.

**Specific preconditions.** A distinction between task instructions, enforceable obligations and an engineering process.

**Actor and authority boundary.** Framework authors state scope; engineers and users assess the actual system rather than its role names.

**Observable postcondition.** Claims of engineering coverage identify actual prescriptions and obligations beyond prompt wording.

**Important criticisms.** **Criticism ids.** C18

**Source ids.** EAOSE-CS101; EAOSE-CS093

**Researcher objection.** Frameworks may offer useful mechanisms, so rejecting the universal inference does not reject their use. The role critique itself is not comparative proof for every proposed alternative.

**Known failure modes.** Frameworks may offer useful mechanisms, so rejecting the universal inference does not reject their use. The role critique itself is not comparative proof for every proposed alternative.

**Contrary evidence.** **Criticism ids.** C18

**Detail.** Frameworks may offer useful mechanisms, so rejecting the universal inference does not reject their use. The role critique itself is not comparative proof for every proposed alternative.

**Open questions.** The universal or sufficient-condition claim is not warranted; its rejection does not reject all bounded uses of the underlying mechanism.

**Disposition rationale.** The input judgement is retained at the exact canonical bearer, polarity and trigger. A rejects role prompts alone as a whole AOSE engineering method. B-072 targets formal role contracts, not complete lifecycle methodology, so it cannot replace this adverse claim.
